Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). 5. Exit codes 1 and 129 (SIGHUP) suggest the job was killed, possibly due to resource constraints.

```text
Looking at the log structure more carefully, it appears two separate log streams are overlapping, making it hard to trace the exact sequence of events.

The traceback and progress bars are interleaved, which could mean either a failed attempt followed by a retry that reached 20% before termination, or two processes writing to the same log file. The "Terminated" signal with EXIT_CODE=129 points to external termination rather than a crash.

I need to check the registry state — earlier I saw plan-002-formal-v6 marked as ALLOCATED with the hypothesis about removing class_weights, but I should verify if that entry still exists or if something changed.

Looking at the actual log output, v6 clearly started training with the data loaded correctly (2040 samples across 11 files), but then hit a traceback in WeightedCETrainer.__init__, which is strange since v6 was supposed to remove class_weights entirely. The process made it to around 20% before getting terminated. The traceback continues through the transformers library deprecation wrapper, suggesting the error might stem from a deprecated parameter being passed to the parent trainer class. The logs seem to show two separate runs—the first one crashed with this WeightedCETrainer initialization error, then a second run started and progressed to 20% before being terminated, likely due to OOM or a manual kill. I need to verify whether class_weights was actually removed from the v6 code and why WeightedCETrainer is still being instantiated if that parameter no longer exists.
```

```text
Looking at the full v6 command now, I can confirm there's no `--class_weights auto` flag, so it was removed. However, the code still instantiates WeightedCETrainer, which means the default behavior when class_weights isn't specified is critical — I should flag that the Agent needs to verify the code falls back to standard CE loss (weight=None) rather than some other default behavior.

But now examining the dryrun command, it also lacks the `--class_weights` flag and uses the same script, yet the dryrun notes claim class_weights=[11.07, 0.52] and WeightedRandomSampler were both active. The notes were later corrected to indicate these were indeed applied through WeightedCETrainer.compute_loss overriding the default behavior — so even without the explicit flag, the weighted mechanisms were somehow engaged during the dryrun.

This creates a puzzle: if class_weights were active in the dryrun and the loss was normal (0.63), why does the v3/v4/v5 diagnosis blame class_weights as the root cause? The key difference might be the model path — the dryrun doesn't specify `--model_name`, defaulting to HF cache, while v3-v5 explicitly point to `/root/autodl-tmp/models/Qwen/Qwen3-1.7B` on ModelScope. That could be the actual confound here.

But then plan-004-ablation-b (no class_weights, ModelScope model) showed normal loss, while plan-004-v2 (with class_weights, ModelScope model) showed abnormal loss — which points back to class_weights being the culprit. There's a contradiction I need to resolve.

The real issue is that plan-004 uses a completely different training script and task than the dryrun, so I can't cleanly compare them. What I can verify now is that the first attempt failed due to a code error, the retry is running and looks healthy, and the retry correctly removes the `--class_weights` flag. I need to wait for it to complete to confirm whether this fixes the loss issue.

But I'm realizing I should double-check something: the dryrun notes claim class_weights were applied, but the actual dryrun command doesn't include `--class_weights auto`. If class_weights are applied by default in the code itself, then v6 would also apply them by default, which would undermine the whole test.

Looking at the actual weight values tells me something important — the dryrun had [11.07, 0.52] while v3-v5 had [4.5, 0.5625]. These are different because the dryrun used 10 tasks with 5880 samples while v3-v5 used 11 tasks with 2040 samples, so the class distributions changed. This suggests the weights are computed dynamically from the data. If the code applies class_weights by default, then v6 without the flag might still apply them unless the code was explicitly modified to skip them. <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>
```

```text
Now I can see the code logic:

1. Line 74: `--class_weights` default is `None`
2. Line 430-432: `class_weights = None; if args.class_weights is not None: class_weights = compute_class_weights(...)`
3. Line 259-262: WeightedCETrainer takes `class_weights: Optional[torch.Tensor] = None`, stores as `self._class_weights`
4. Line 269-270: In compute_loss, only applies weighted CE if `self._class_weights is not None`
5. Line 499: Passes `class_weights=class_weights` to WeightedCETrainer


6. When `--class_weights` isn't passed, the None value flows through the entire pipeline and defaults to standard cross-entropy loss, so v6 correctly disables class weighting.

But the dryrun notes mentioned class_weights=[11.07, 0.52] being applied without the `--class_weights` flag, which doesn't match this code path — either the dryrun used a different version or the notes were inaccurate. The dryrun command in the registry was likely incomplete or the Agent passed class_weights differently than what's captured there, but the current code correctly defaults to `None` when the flag is absent, so v6's experiment design is sound. The first failure was actually a transformers library API change in the trainer initialization, not related to class_weights removal—the Agent fixed it on retry. I also notice the GPU allocation shifted from cuda:1 to cuda:0 via CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES, which isn't a constraint violation but worth noting.

Looking at the logs, the first run failed with EXIT_CODE=1 and logged to ~/runs, while the retry is running cleanly from the checkpoint directory at step 17/153. The core issue was transient—a code/library incompatibility, not an experiment design flaw. The retry should finish in roughly 15 minutes, at which point I can verify whether the loss normalization hypothesis holds.
```

```text
I have two worker completions to review. Let me check the current state of the registry and any updates. I also have pending analysis from plan-004-v3's trainer_state.json that I pulled earlier but haven't reported on yet.

From the trainer_state.json I read earlier for plan-004-v3:
- Loss curve: 48.15 → 42.05 → 26.14 → 13.73 → 8.47 → 6.84 → 6.05 → 5.89 → 5.03 → 4.58 → 4.42 → 4.15 → 4.47 → 4.37


- Eval loss shows improvement across epochs: 0.953 → 0.553 → 0.491
- Gradient norms are stabilizing: 122 → 131 → 92 → 52 → 37 → 25 → 15 → 16 → 10 → 10 → 16 → 14 → 11 → 14

Wait, this is concerning—plan-004-v3 was supposed to remove class_weights, but the training loss starts at 48.15 and plateaus around 4-5 by epoch 3, which looks identical to the previous run.

Let me check the config again. The train_config.json definitely shows class_weights: null, but the loss trajectory is nearly identical to v2 which had class_weights set to auto. Comparing epoch 1 losses directly: v2 goes 48.15 → 42.05 → 26.14 → 13.71, and v3 goes 48.15 → 42.05 → 26.14 → 13.73. These are practically the same, which means removing class_weights didn't actually change anything about the loss anomaly in the implicit router script. The loss values being identical despite different class_weights settings is puzzling — it suggests the weighting branch isn't even being triggered, or the real issue lies elsewhere in the pipeline. Looking at the implicit script's code, I see the class_weights scaling only applies when both the weights and sample indices exist, otherwise it falls back to standard loss computation. But here's the thing: the implicit script uses a generative approach with cross-entropy over token outputs, not a classification head, so a loss of 48 might actually be reasonable for that task.

Wait, that doesn't match the original plan-004-ablation-b run on the same script which got 0.36-0.71 loss. Something fundamental changed between those two runs. The data path is the most suspicious difference — ablation-b used the local data directory while v3 is pulling from a results folder with different formatting, which could easily explain the massive loss spike.

The worker name "worker_code_plan-004-v3-data-fix-train" is interesting because it explicitly mentions "data-fix" — so the worker was supposed to address data issues, but I need to check the registry to see what actually happened. I should also look into the status of plan-002-formal-v6 since another worker was investigating its crash.
```

```text
The loss drops sharply through the first epoch, then continues declining more gradually through epoch 2, with gradient norms stabilizing around 10-16 by the end.

Looking at the next batch of metrics, the training loss continues its downward trajectory through epochs 2-3, and the eval losses are dropping significantly—from 0.953 down to 0.491. This is matching the exact same pattern I saw in plan-004-v2, which used class weights, so the approach is clearly working well. Looking at the step-by-step comparison between the two versions, the results are virtually identical across all checkpoints. This is a crucial finding—removing class_weights made no difference to the implicit router's training, which means that hypothesis doesn't explain the abnormally high loss values that drop from 48 down to around 4.4.

But I need to step back and compare this against the original working version. The plan-004-ablation-b run used the same script without class_weights and achieved normal loss progression (0.71→0.39→0.36), with reasonable eval metrics. The key difference isn't the class_weights parameter—it's the data itself. The working version used upsampled minority data from the original implicit directory, while the current v3 is training on the test labels dataset without any upsampling. That data distribution shift is likely what's driving the unusual loss curve. The identical loss values between v2 and v3 suggest class_weights might never have been applied in v2 due to a code issue. Looking at the implicit_router_sft.py implementation, class_weights are applied per-sample only when `sample_indices` is not None, which is a different approach than the standard CrossEntropyLoss weighting—so if the Trainer doesn't pass sample indices correctly, the weights would be silently skipped even when set.

The real puzzle is why the implicit script shows drastically different loss curves (48→4.4 vs 0.71→0.36) depending on whether it's using plan-004-implicit or plan_c_test_labels data, which suggests the root cause isn't class_weights at all but rather a fundamental difference in the data files themselves or how they're formatted.

This makes me question whether class_weights is even the culprit for the explicit router—if removing it from the implicit script had zero effect, maybe the explicit router's problem lies elsewhere too. I should wait for the formal v6 results to see if that hypothesis holds up.

Actually, thinking about this more carefully: the identical loss values between implicit v2 and v3 strongly suggest class_weights was never actually applied in that script due to a bug. So the implicit script's high loss must stem from something else, probably the data itself.

The explicit script does apply class_weights differently through the CrossEntropyLoss weight parameter, so it could still be the issue there. But now I'm noticing something critical—ablation-b and v2/v3 are pulling from completely different data directories. The ablation uses `data/plan-004-implicit/` while v2 and v3 use `results/plan_c_test_labels/implicit_sft.jsonl`. This data mismatch is almost certainly the real culprit, not class_weights at all. The agent diagnosed this as a class_weights problem and spun up v3 as a fix, but the identical results prove that was wrong. This misdiagnosis is burning GPU resources and could lead me down the wrong path entirely. The worker's diagnosis that class_weights caused the failure was reasonable at the time, but v3's identical loss results prove this wrong—class_weights isn't actually the active variable in the implicit script. Actually, I need to reconsider what's happening. The implicit_router_sft.py uses a generative SFT approach rather than a classification head, so the loss function is standard causal language modeling loss computed across the entire sequence. A starting loss of 48 might be perfectly normal for a long sequence when the model hasn't learned this format yet. I should compare this against the original plan-004-ablation-b to see if this loss trajectory is actually expected.
```

```text
Now I'm wondering about that initial loss of 48. The implicit_router_sft.py script uses a generative approach where the Trainer logs average per-token cross-entropy, and 48 per token is impossibly high for a language model. I'm checking the code to see if there's custom loss computation happening—the class_weights scaling only applies when class_weights is not None, so when it's None the loss should come directly from the parent Trainer. Wait, I should look at what changed between the ablation-b and v3 runs.
```
